- 04:5xZ (2026-10-20; 00:25 local) — **THE OPERATOR on the footer line:** "is it neccessary, does it have legal merit, and what is the standard, as i have never seen others do it." ## INC-785 — LGA-13 split the acceptances export on "\n" though its lines end in "\r\n" (2026-10-20; test — e2e/legal-admin.spec.ts :559 at `cf1dc2ed`; found on run 1060, both projects)

Defect: the export writes RFC 4180 line endings (src/features/admin/legal/legal-service.ts :334 `join("\r\n") + "\r\n"`, turn 13's carrier); LGA-13 split the downloaded file on "\n", so its first line kept a trailing "\r" and `toBe(header)` failed while both printed the same text. The export is right. Fix: turn 14b A4 — assert the CRLF ending, no bare "\n", and split on "\r\n"; nothing loosened. Census (G29): the only e2e split of a downloaded file. Class: a test reading a file must read it in the line ending the file is specified to have (S335).

- **S335 (the supervisor's slip):** turn 13 v2's C4 told the executor "its first line is exactly …" while its own carrier set the file's lines to end in "\r\n", without saying how the test reads lines — the test was written the natural way and failed in CI (INC-785). Class: a brief that prescribes a file format and a test of it names the format's separator in the test's text.

## INC-786 — the view counter's insert fails with a foreign-key error when the listing is deleted while it is viewed (2026-10-20; server — /api/listings/view, table listing_counters; found by run 1061's server census, shard 1)

Defect: "[ssr-error] /api/listings/view insert or update on table "listing_counters" violates foreign key constraint "listing_counters_listing_id_fkey"" — a view of a listing that was deleted between the page and the beacon (a test's teardown beside AP-35 / AT-51) reaches the server as a 5xx-class error instead of "listing gone". Evidence: run 1061 e2e-last-failure.md, server census, 1 line. Fix: owed to the flake-and-cleanup turn — the view door treats a missing listing (the foreign-key refusal, SQLSTATE 23503) as the same quiet outcome as a listing not found, with a test that deletes the listing before the beacon; census (G29) of every door that writes a row keyed by listing_id after a read. Class: a write keyed by a row another actor can delete must treat the delete as a state, not an error.

Turn 14c says the tool in whatever form is right and that the old words meant the `patch` / `git apply` commands.

## INC-787 — main cannot be promoted: GitHub refuses the promote job's push of a range that adds a workflow file (2026-10-20; CI — .github/workflows/ci.yml promote job :1178-:1193; found by run 1062)

Defect: run 1062 (7464c799) passed every test, and its promote job failed: "refusing to allow a GitHub App to create or update workflow `.github/workflows/local-backend-proof.yml` without `workflows` permission". The job pushes with the workflow's GITHUB_TOKEN (contents: write), which has no `workflows` permission; turn 14 added the proof workflow, so main (38b9e6d6) cannot fast-forward while the range carries it, and every run ends FAILURE by promote alone. The same kind of push succeeded on 2026-10-17 (run 969, flake-check.yml); why it is refused now is unknown (HYPOTHESIS: a GitHub-side enforcement change). Evidence: guards-last-failure.md of run 1062 (the promote job's tail). Fix: a credential with the `workflows` permission for the promote job only — the operator's decision (a security trade-off, G17); then one turn wires it. Class: a job that pushes code must hold every permission the pushed range can need, including workflow files.

- FV-1 in run 1062 (7464c799, turn 14 v2's poll of gapY): three retried passes, each "Received: 34" on the vehicles row after a 10-s poll — the one-row (34-px) state ALSO persists, so neither offset is motion. Both observed states (34 px, 1.67 px) point to the two lists differing in their rows (count or heights) — INC-784's hypothesis gains weight; the Flake check run 6 on a4dee36c (rowPair diagnosis) will show the rows.

(3) **FLAKE CHECK run 6 (39392539925, a4dee36c; 09:22Z; ran beside run 1063): FAILURE, 38 passed, 24 skipped, 10 failed — and the diagnosis is DECISIVE.** Every failure's JSON shows the two lists holding DIFFERENT ROW SETS: the strip and the menu each list the real main categories plus other tests' scratch categories ("e2e-post-…", made by posting tests running in CI beside it), but not the same ones — e.g. FV-1: the strip 19 rows (one more scratch row, e2e-post-1-1-zrdnyd) at 32 px each (the min-h-8 floor), the menu 18 rows at 32.83 px; RM-1: the menu 19 rows at 32 px, the strip 17 rows at 35.06 px. Rows are flex-1 in a 628-px container, so a different row count gives a different row height, and the offset grows by row index (1.67, 2.05, 3.05 px; 34 px = a whole row). **ROOT CAUSE (G3: read, not guessed — src/features/categories/category-tree.ts :99-:106, :291-:305, :400-:434):** useCategoryTree keeps the tree in each mount's OWN React state, seeded from the module cache and revalidated on mount ("every mount asks", INC-263/INC-265); loadCategoryTree updates the module cache but tells no other mount. The strip mounts with the page; the menu (dialog content) mounts when opened and fetches again — so when the catalogue changed in between, the menu shows the new tree and the strip keeps the old one until a reload. A product defect, not a test defect: two readers on one page can show different versions of the catalogue (the menu row over the strip row opens a different category than the row painted — D205), and the same holds for every one of the fifteen consumers (feed, cards, crumbs, wizard, admin pages). → **INC-788**; INC-773 and INC-784 are its symptoms (close when the fix is proven). Fix: the held tree becomes one subscribed store (useSyncExternalStore over the module cache, as the favourite store does, app-rail.tsx :47-:90): every mount still asks; a moved version re-renders every reader at once; an unmoved one hands back the same object.

## INC-788 — two readers of the category tree on one page can hold different versions: each mount keeps its own copy (2026-10-20; product — src/features/categories/category-tree.ts useCategoryTree :400-:434, loadCategoryTree :291-:305 at `3f064e01`; found by the Flake check's diagnosis (turn 14c's rowPair), run 39392539925)

Defect: useCategoryTree holds the tree in per-mount state; a mount made later (the phone menu, opened after the strip) revalidates and sees a newer catalogue while earlier mounts keep the older copy; nothing tells them. Evidence: the Flake check's failure bodies — the strip and the menu list different scratch categories (19 vs 18, 17 vs 19 rows), so flex-filled row heights differ (32 vs 32.83, 35.06 vs 32 px) and the offset grows down the list (INC-773 FV-1, INC-784 RM-1, "the menu opens in place"). Fix: one shared, subscribed store for the held tree (every mount asks; every mount sees the newest version; same object when unmoved); unit test (two mounts; the later one's revalidation updates the earlier one), shown red against the current code; then the Flake check of the three tests. Class: a value shared across a page is held in one store that every reader subscribes to, never copied into each reader's state.

## INC-789 — the temporary backend's API answered over http, and the app keeps only https photo links: every stored photo read as "no photo" (2026-10-20; harness — .github/workflows/local-backend-proof.yml; found by proof #7, 13 tests, both projects)

Defect: the CLI serves the local API at http://127.0.0.1:54321; the app's photo readers keep a link only when it begins "https://" (photo-links.ts :29 and :43 at `78b96e25`, a product rule: no http image on an https site), so every signed link was dropped: the gallery drew no thumbnails (AP-9, AP-48), no main photo (AP-36, AP-50, PP-22, PP-23), the card read "No photo yet" (FS-15, PP-21), the photo step drew nothing and its Edit button stayed disabled (PP-12, PP-27, PP-28), and the two tests that read the link printed it (AP-19, AP-49). Class: an environment that differs from production in a property the product relies on — fixed in the environment, never by relaxing the product rule or a test. Fix (turn 15c B): a per-run certificate authority made in the job (its key deleted once the API certificate is signed); the CLI's own TLS for the API (`[api.tls]` with cert_path and key_path, CLI 2.120.0 pkg/config — the external URL becomes https); Chromium trusts the CA through the runner's NSS database ($HOME/.pki/nssdb) and Node and Bun through NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS; the three exact-URL guards (the workflow's status parse :301, e2e/global-setup.ts :405, e2e/helpers/app-target.ts :18-:19 with src/test/app-target.test.ts) accept https://127.0.0.1:54321 only. Grounding (the supervisor's workspace, 11:3xZ): Node 22 and Bun 1.3.14 refuse the private CA without the variable (UNABLE_TO_VERIFY_LEAF_SIGNATURE) and accept it with it; Playwright's Chromium build 1194 (headless shell and full) refuses it with no trust (ERR_CERT_AUTHORITY_INVALID) and with the CA in the NSS database loads the page, an image from an http page and APIRequestContext all 200, with no ignore flag. Status: registered; fix in turn 15c.

## INC-790 — the languages page judged a language's publication by its own rows, not by the base catalogue: a language with no rows read publishable while the door refuses (2026-10-20; product — src/features/admin/translations/languages-page.tsx coverageOf :176-:180, the public cell :501-:509 at `78b96e25`; found by proof #7, TR-6 both projects)

Defect: the door (admin_set_language_flags, 20260901234603) refuses publication unless the language holds an approved, non-null row for every live key of the BASE catalogue; the page's gate computes remaining = (the language's own live rows) − (its approved rows). A language holding no rows (never synced for it — om on the temporary backend, where only the scratch key's en and am rows exist) reads 0 of 0, its switch is enabled, and the click is refused by the door. Staging hides it: its sync wrote om's 2,938 untranslated rows. Class: a completeness judgement measured over the wrong universe — the EMPTY-SET LAW (INC-095h) applied to the base catalogue but not to a language's own rows. Fix (turn 15c C): one pure judgement beside TranslationStats — every non-base language measured against the base catalogue's live keys (total = base, approved capped at base, remaining = base − approved); the gate and both meters read it; the base language keeps its own. Residual, named: the stats count an approved row with a null value as approved where the door does not. Status: registered; fix in turn 15c.

## INC-791 — PW-162 (d): a Next's claim went out twice in one press again — a save run queued before the Next carried it (2026-10-20; product — src/features/posting/use-draft.ts flush :479-:492, the follow-up timer :380-:390, every run queued from a timer or a change; found by proof #7, both projects)

Defect: (d) aborts the first step-4 send; the evidence shows sends [step 2 sent, step 4 aborted, step 4 sent, step 4 sent] and the wizard on step 5 — the claim was sent twice and the second send advanced it. HYPOTHESIS (G3; the queue read verbatim at `78b96e25`, the timing not visible): a run queued BEFORE the Next — the 0-ms follow-up timer armed by an autosave answered before the press (:387-:389), or a debounce, change, retry or pause run waiting behind an in-flight save — reaches the queue first after the press; `pass` reads strictRef and carries the claim; its transport failure is not charged to the claim, and the Next's own run sends it again; a follow-up flag set in a run's last pass also outlives that run (flush :482 checks once). INC-698 closed one path (a superseded answer armed nothing); this is the class at its second occurrence. Fix (turn 15c D): every run notes the claim count when it is queued and sends nothing when a newer claim was made since; follow-ups are drained inside the run that got the answer (no 0-ms timer); both shown red-first in unit cases with positive controls. Class rule (AGENTS.md, turn 15c): a run armed before a Next never carries it. Status: registered; fix in turn 15c.

## INC-792 — the attributes import spec outpaces the door's per-user preview budget on a faster backend (2026-10-20; harness — e2e/admin-attributes-import.spec.ts importPost :107-:123 and the other preview senders; found by proof #7: AT-27 failed, AT-70 and AT-71 retried, all 429 tooManyRequests)

Defect: the gate allows 30 previews a minute per user and family, in server memory (src/server/imports/gate.ts :792, :807-:828); the spec's twenty tests run serially on one pooled identity and send 33 or more previews, under a minute on the temporary backend (staging's latency kept them under). Class: a suite on one identity outpacing a per-user server budget. The budget is a DoS ceiling and is never raised for a test (G38); a refused preview costs nothing (refused before the plan and the budget count). Fix (turn 15c E): `withinPreviewBudget` beside `inOneWindow` in e2e/helpers/rate-window.ts — on 429 tooManyRequests it sends the same request again every 5 s, at most 65 s, and grows the test's own budget by that wait (its named cause, G38); every preview sender on a pooled identity goes through it; IG-3's limit loop (a minted identity asserting the 429) and the anonymous posts stay raw. ## INC-793 — a draft save below the price step reached listings_price_pair_check: a 500-class door error answered as doorError (2026-10-20; product door — validate_listing_draft 20261004144146 output :401-:402 and its step-below-4 branch; found by proof #7's server census, shards 5 and 11, during PW-61/PW-73)

## INC-794 — the proof lane masked its results file as raw text, and the e-mail rule broke the JSON: lane 3's failure body was lost (2026-10-20; harness — .github/workflows/local-backend-proof.yml step "Write lane evidence" :557-:559 at `6baac306`; found by proof #8, lane 3)

Defect: after counting from test-results/results.json, the step rewrites it with `artifact.write_text(redact(artifact.read_text(errors='replace')))`; redact's e-mail rule `[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}` also matches the letter of a JSON escape before an address: `"line\nuser@example.invalid"` becomes `"line\[e-mail]"`, an invalid escape. Proven in the supervisor's workspace (Python: "Invalid \escape: line 1 column 12"). The render-leak error carries 2,000 characters of the page's text (e2e/helpers/ui.ts :156), and the staff ad page shows the seller's e-mail on its own line, so LGC-3's failure body is exactly the breaking input; the lane's own counts were right because Python parsed the file before masking it; turn 15b made the masked file the reporter's input. Class: a text-level mask applied to a structured file. Fix (turn 15d): the results file is masked as data — every string value and key redacted, the file written back with json.dumps — with a self-check on a synthetic escape-before-address string; census of every mask over a structured file (G29). Status: registered; fix in turn 15d.

- **S344 (the supervisor's slip):** turn 15b's brief made the lane's masked results file the reporter's input ("which already masks its log and results in place … every file a lane uploads passes through the same redact() first") without checking that a regex mask keeps a JSON file valid — the class INC-794 names; first occurrence.

## INC-797 — two fixture factories navigate before the public tree carries their scratch places: MLA-15, MLA-17 and AP-41 fail their first attempt in every temporary-backend proof (2026-10-20; tests — e2e/my-listings-ad.spec.ts scratchSeller :209-:221 and its second chains :1099, :1275; e2e/helpers/ad-page.ts scratchSeller :56-:75; found by proofs #7–#11, and staging's flake ledger: MLA-15 ×3, MLA-17 ×4, AP-41 ×3 in seven days)

Defect: seedScratchChain inserts the chain through the service client and returns; the factories then sign in and open a page whose place reading comes from /api/locations/ET, which serves its in-process copy for up to 15 s (VERSION_TTL_MS). The first attempt meets a tree without the scratch city; the retry, a minute later, passes. Class: seed-before-navigate without waiting on the route (J7; the rule written at locations.ts :470-:474 and kept by about 50 other call sites through waitForTreeSlug). Fix (turn 15g): a node-side wait on the served tree for the chain's region, city and sub-city (readServedTree, bounded 20 s, named), called by every seedScratchChain caller that navigates without its own wait; census of the 43 calls (G29). Status: registered; fix in turn 15g.

## INC-799 — the staff history's reason line has no guard for a reason without a label (2026-10-20; product — src/features/admin-listings/listing-page.tsx :707-:716 at `54dbf7ad`; found by turn 15f's census, the one open site)

Defect: the history prints `t(`admin.listings.reason.${entry.reason}`)`; every reason written today has its key, but the value comes from the database through more than one door, and `t` returns undefined for a key in neither catalogue, so a future reason would read "Reason: undefined" (INC-795's class). Status: registered; fix in turn 15i.

Applied to /home/claude/b22/legal-final (Terms main points + §1 ×3; Privacy §3), EN and AM; check_am.py OK on all three pairs.

Baseline function_executable_by_anon 13 → the live number (expected 19). Closes INC-831 and promotes INC-521's class rule to a CI guard (six recurrences).
  **DEC-241 — a deactivated caller is refused by ONE function, and a census guard refuses a door without it.** public.account_is_deactivated(uuid) (the read) + public.assert_caller_active() (the refusal, 'account is deactivated'); rate_gate and has_permission redeclared to call them; the nine ungated doors gain the call; delete_draft, confirm_home_country, promote_to_super_admin lose EXECUTE for authenticated (INC-836 part 1); scripts/check-caller-gates.ts over the live catalogue in the smoke lane (gated = definition names assert_caller_active( | rate_gate( | has_permission( | is_super_admin(), allowlist `fn | reason | until` (G55 format), fixture-proven. Supervisor's call under G17 (mechanism shape, not product scope), reported in one line.
  **DEC-242 — the password-removal proof.** remove_own_password requires public.require_recent_password(): a password sign-in recorded by GoTrue (auth.mfa_amr_claims.authentication_method = 'password') on ANOTHER session of the same account within 5 minutes; the door consumes that session; rate dial password_remove (5/hour); audit row account.password_removed. The client keeps its throwaway verifier session alive until the door answers (today it signs it out first — auth-service.ts :322-:326). Alternatives weighed: (b) the door verifies the password itself by bcrypt — puts the password in an RPC body and opens a brute-force surface behind a stolen token; (c) re-sign-in on the main client — replaces an MFA user's aal2 session; (a) chosen: no password in SQL, GoTrue's own rate limit guards the proof, same proof shape as require_step_up_if_needed. Window: between the apply and the next Publish, Remove password on the live site is refused (the published client signs the proof out first); the stop report tells the operator. Security design, decided by Claude and reported as overridable (G17; §3 PASTE AS APPROVAL lists it).
  INC-833's fix shape therefore differs from the audit synthesis (trigger clause → one function + census guard); the synthesis doc is not rewritten — the record notes it here and the records turn carries DEC-241.
